# RODET: Arabic for the compiler's messages, shown when RODET_LANG=ar.
#
# Each message: `en:` the exact English text from the compiler's source, then
# `ar:` its Arabic. Keep `{$name}` holes (Fluent messages) as they are; in
# messages built with format!, `{}` / `{0}` `{1}`… are the values in order.
# Fluent selectors keep their shape: {$n -> [one] … *[other] …}.
# Code, names and types stay in `backticks`.
#
# What is missing: python3 rodet/i18n/compiler_messages.py missing [crate]

en: cannot cast thin pointer `{$expr_ty}` to wide pointer `{$cast_ty}`
ar: لا يمكن تحويل المؤشر البسيط `{$expr_ty}` إلى المؤشر العريض `{$cast_ty}`

en: thin pointers are "simple" pointers: they are purely a reference to a\n        memory address.\n\n        Wide pointers are pointers referencing "Dynamically Sized Types" (also\n        called DST). DST don't have a statically known size, therefore they can\n        only exist behind some kind of pointers that contain additional\n        information. Slices and trait objects are DSTs. In the case of slices,\n        the additional information the wide pointer holds is their size.\n\n        To fix this error, don't try to cast directly between thin and wide\n        pointers.\n\n        For more information about casts, take a look at The Book:\n        https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/expressions/operator-expr.html#type-cast-expressions
ar: المؤشرات البسيطة (thin) تشير فقط إلى عنوان في الذاكرة.\n\n    المؤشرات العريضة (wide) تشير إلى أنواع غير معروفة الحجم (DST)، ولأن حجمها غير معروف وقت الترجمة فلا توجد إلا خلف مؤشر يحمل معلومات إضافية. الشرائح (slices) وكائنات الـ trait من هذه الأنواع؛ وفي الشرائح تكون المعلومة الإضافية هي الطول.\n\n    لإصلاح هذا الخطأ لا تحوّل مباشرة بين المؤشرات البسيطة والعريضة.\n\n    لمزيد عن التحويلات: https://doc.rust-lang.org/reference/expressions/operator-expr.html#type-cast-expressions

en: if you are in a merge, the top section is the code you already had checked out and the bottom section is the new code\nif you are in a rebase, the top section is the code being rebased onto and the bottom section is the code you had checked out which is being rebased

en: In a match expression, only numbers and characters can be matched against a range. This is because the compiler checks that the range is non-empty at compile-time, and is unable to evaluate arbitrary comparison functions. If you want to capture values of an orderable type between two end-points, you can use a guard.
